Now it’s like I’m there, but I don’t really feel connected to it. I kind of just stand there sometimes like… I don’t care about any of it the way I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. But I keep thinking maybe it’s just me being worn down or having a rough time. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the “nearly every day” part… I mean, it has been going on for a while, yeah, and most days feel pretty low. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not just like one bad afternoon.\n\nFor the past couple weeks or more, I guess I have been feeling down a lot of the day. And sometimes I barely did anything earlier that day, so it doesn’t really make sense to me. I keep trying to push through it, but… yeah, I guess my energy’s been lower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself having trouble getting started on tasks or feeling like you can't get going, even when you know you need to do something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy or anything.\n\nIt’s more like once I start doing normal stuff at work, even carrying a couple cases of liquor or setting up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer get any pleasure out of things you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but nothing really feels like… mine anymore, if that makes sense. Now I kind of just stand there, and it’s like I don’t care about any of it. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s pretty different. Those things used to feel like mine, you know? Murals, going for runs, playing basketball… even making a good drink at work could feel satisfying.\n\nNow I kind of just… do it or stand there, and there’s not much there. Like I don’t really care about any of it the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say “depressed” exactly. I mean, I know I haven’t been myself, and I do feel kind of low a lot of the time, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nMost days feel pretty heavy, I guess. But maybe I’m just tired or in a bad stretch. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nThose things used to matter to me—painting murals, going for runs, basketball, even just making a good drink at work. Now it’s like I’m there, but I don’t really feel connected to it. I kind of just stand there sometimes like… I don’t care about any of it the way I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I’m not trying to avoid the question, I just don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nMost days I do feel pretty low, yeah. It’s kind of there a lot of the day, like a heaviness. But I keep thinking maybe it’s just me being worn down or having a rough time. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the “nearly every day” part… I mean, it has been going on for a while, yeah, and most days feel pretty low. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not just like one bad afternoon.\n\nFor the past couple weeks or more, I guess I have been feeling down a lot of the day. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels lately, like feeling more tired or having less energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I have noticed that.\n\nAt work, even just carrying a couple cases of liquor or setting up the well, my body feels like I already worked a double. And sometimes I barely did anything earlier that day, so it doesn’t really make sense to me. I keep trying to push through it, but… yeah, I guess my energy’s been lower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself having trouble getting started on tasks or feeling like you can't get going, even when you know you need to do something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy or anything.\n\nIt’s more like once I start doing normal stuff at work, even carrying a couple cases of liquor or setting up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double. And sometimes I barely did anything before that, so I don’t really get why I feel so worn out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer get any pleasure out of things you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but nothing really feels like… mine anymore, if that makes sense. I used to get excited about murals, going for runs, playing basketball, even just making a really good drink at the bar. Now I’ll try to do it and I’m just sort of standing there, like I don’t care about any of it.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I just… I don’t get any real feeling from it the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you feel when you try to do the things you used to enjoy, like painting or playing basketball? I used to get excited about murals, going for a run, even just making a really good drink at work. Now it’s more like I’m just… standing there doing the motions, and it doesn’t feel like it belongs to me anymore.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but there’s not really any reward in it lately. I just don’t care the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that for the past two weeks or more, you've felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I’ve had low days, sure, but I don’t know if I’d say most of the day, every day for two weeks. I haven’t really been keeping track like that.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It’s more like I notice something’s off, but pinning it down to that specific amount of time is hard for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've had days where you felt down or low for most of the day, and if so, how often has that happened over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: I guess… there have been some days like that, yeah. I’m sorry, I’m not trying to be vague.\n\nIt’s hard for me to say how often exactly. I haven’t really been marking it down or anything. Some days I can get through work and seem okay, and then other days it’s like this low feeling is just sitting there most of the day.\n\nOver the past few weeks… maybe more days than I’d like to admit, but I don’t know if I can give you a clean number. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, there have been at least several days where you felt down or low for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think “several days” is probably fair. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… hi. I guess I’m here because things just don’t feel like they used to. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but… nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nI used to get excited about stuff—painting murals, going for runs, playing basketball, even just making a really good drink at work. Now I kind of just stand there, and it’s like I don’t care about any of it. I’m not sure if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in how you feel about the things you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s pretty different. Those things used to feel like mine, you know? Murals, going for runs, playing basketball… even making a good drink at work could feel satisfying.\n\nNow I kind of just… do it or stand there, and there’s not much there. Like I don’t really care about any of it the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say “depressed” exactly. I mean, I know I haven’t been myself, and I do feel kind of low a lot of the time, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nMost days feel pretty heavy, I guess. But maybe I’m just tired or in a bad stretch. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nThose things used to matter to me—painting murals, going for runs, basketball, even just making a good drink at work. Now it’s like I’m there, but I don’t really feel connected to it. I kind of just stand there sometimes like… I don’t care about any of it the way I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I’m not trying to avoid the question, I just don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nMost days I do feel pretty low, yeah. It’s kind of there a lot of the day, like a heaviness. But I keep thinking maybe it’s just me being worn down or having a rough time. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the “nearly every day” part… I mean, it has been going on for a while, yeah, and most days feel pretty low. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not just like one bad afternoon.\n\nFor the past couple weeks or more, I guess I have been feeling down a lot of the day. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your energy levels lately, like feeling more tired or having less energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I have noticed that.\n\nAt work, even just carrying a couple cases of liquor or setting up the well, my body feels like I already worked a double. And sometimes I barely did anything earlier that day, so it doesn’t really make sense to me. I keep trying to push through it, but… yeah, I guess my energy’s been lower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself having trouble getting started on tasks or feeling like you can't get going, even when you know you need to do something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy or anything.\n\nIt’s more like once I start doing normal stuff at work, even carrying a couple cases of liquor or setting up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double. And sometimes I barely did anything before that, so I don’t really get why I feel so worn out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer get any pleasure out of things you used to enjoy, like painting murals or playing basketball, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but nothing really feels like… mine anymore, if that makes sense. I used to get excited about murals, going for runs, playing basketball, even just making a really good drink at the bar. Now I’ll try to do it and I’m just sort of standing there, like I don’t care about any of it.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I just… I don’t get any real feeling from it the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you feel when you try to do the things you used to enjoy, like painting or playing basketball? Do they feel completely empty or without any reward now?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think “empty” is probably the right word, even if I don’t like saying it that way.\n\nLike with painting, or basketball, I’ll try to make myself do it because I know I used to care about it. I used to get excited about murals, going for a run, even just making a really good drink at work. Now it’s more like I’m just… standing there doing the motions, and it doesn’t feel like it belongs to me anymore.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but there’s not really any reward in it lately. I just don’t care the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that for the past two weeks or more, you've felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I’ve had low days, sure, but I don’t know if I’d say most of the day, every day for two weeks. I haven’t really been keeping track like that.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It’s more like I notice something’s off, but pinning it down to that specific amount of time is hard for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've had days where you felt down or low for most of the day, and if so, how often has that happened over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: I guess… there have been some days like that, yeah. I’m sorry, I’m not trying to be vague.\n\nIt’s hard for me to say how often exactly. I haven’t really been marking it down or anything. Some days I can get through work and seem okay, and then other days it’s like this low feeling is just sitting there most of the day.\n\nOver the past few weeks… maybe more days than I’d like to admit, but I don’t know if I can give you a clean number. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that over the past two weeks, there have been at least several days where you felt down or low for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think “several days” is probably fair. I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, there have been days in the last couple weeks where I felt pretty low for a lot of the day.\n\nI’m not sure I’d call it every day, but several days… yeah, I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, for most of the day, you have felt down or low over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say “most days, most of the day.” I mean… maybe it’s closer than I want to admit, but I’m not sure.\n\nThere have definitely been several days where I’ve felt low for a good chunk of the day. I just don’t want to make it sound worse than it is if I’m not totally certain. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on most days, for most of the day, you have felt down or low over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to answer that cleanly. “Most days, most of the day” sounds… pretty strong, and I don’t want to say yes if I’m not certain.\n\nI know there have been several days where it was there for a lot of the day, but whether that counts as most days… I’m not sure. Maybe I’m splitting hairs. Is it okay if I just say I’m not completely sure?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on more days than not, you have felt down or low for most of the day over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I’m sorry, I know I’m not giving you the cleanest answer.\n\n“More days than not” makes it a little clearer, but I’m still not completely sure. I can say there have been several days where I felt pretty low for a lot of the day. Whether it’s actually been more days than not… I’d hate to say yes and make it sound bigger than I can back up.\n\nSo maybe the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. Does that still help?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on more days than not, you have felt down or low for most of the day over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get a yes-or-no from me. I just… I don’t think I can say that for sure.\n\nIt’s been there some days, and sometimes it hangs around longer than I’d like, but “more days than not, most of the day” still feels hard for me to answer honestly. I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe I’d say I’m not sure, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on more days than not, you have felt down or low for most of the day over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry… I know I keep making this harder than it needs to be. I just don’t feel totally confident saying yes to that exact wording.\n\nThere have been low stretches, definitely, but whether it’s been more days than not and most of the day… I don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be agreeable.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on more days than not, you have felt down or low for most of the day over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I think I’m still not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t want to say “yes” if I’m exaggerating it, you know?\n\nThere have been times I’ve felt low, but when you say “more days than not” and “most of the day,” I get stuck. I guess I can’t confidently say that’s been true. Is it okay if I just say I’m not sure?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that on most days, for most of the day, you have felt down or low over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably not giving you the straightforward answer you need. I don’t think I can honestly say yes to “most days, most of the day.”\n\nI’ve had low times, yeah, but I keep second-guessing whether it’s been that much. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. So… I’d say I’m not sure, or maybe not most days. Is that okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
